Passed right by Xiaoman's feet.

Xiaoman held her breath.

You didn't notice.

Went past.

Five minutes.

Ten minutes.

Xiaoman's legs went numb from squatting.

You were still looking.

Finally you came back from the other side of the warehouse.

An ant crawled next to Xiaoman's toe.

You finally looked up.

Saw a giant foot.

Followed the foot up.

Legs.

Skirt.

Hands.

Face.

Xiaoman:

"..."

You said:

"Found you."

Xiaoman:

"You walked past me three times."

"Yeah."

"You didn't see me?"

"Didn't look there."

Xiaoman discovered for the first time.

She was this big.

Could also "disappear" in front of Miaowu.

---

Round two.

You hide.

Three minutes later.

The game stopped being fun.

Because you really were gone.

Xiaoman laughed at first.

"Miaowu——"

No answer.

She looked behind the awning.

No.

Concrete pipe.

No.

Under the abandoned car.

No.

Five minutes later.

She stopped laughing.

"Miaowu?"

Still nothing.

Ten minutes.

She started afraid to walk.

Because you were too small.

She was afraid she'd step somewhere by accident.

"Miaowu!"

Voice a little louder.

Warehouse windows hummed.

Still nothing.

Finally everyone started looking.

Seventeen minutes later.

The high school student found you behind a giant horizontal drainage pipe.

You stood there.

Hugging your knees.

Motionless.

"Why didn't you come out?"

You said:

"You didn't find me."

"We've been looking for ten minutes!"

"Yeah."

"Did you hear Xiaoman calling you?"

"I heard."

"Then why didn't you answer?"

You looked up.

"In hide and seek you can't tell the seeker where you are."

High school student:

"..."

Finally.

One new rule added:

If Xiaoman calls your name three times in a row.

You must answer.

You asked:

"Why?"

Xiaoman hugged her knees.

Eyes still red.

She said:

"Because I'll think you're really gone."

You looked at her for a while.

"Oh."

Round three.

You hid again.

Xiaoman looked for seven minutes.

"Miaowu."

No sound.

"Miaowu."

No.

"Miaowu."

From behind the drainage pipe:

"I'm here."

Xiaoman smiled immediately.

============================================================

## Chapter 48: Hopscotch Without Hopping

**Day 14**

You drew squares on the ground with chalk.

One.

Two.

Three.

Four.

Drew to ten.

Xiaoman squatted beside watching.

"What is this?"

"Hopscotch."

"Can I play?"

Everyone went quiet for a second.

Half an hour later.

A giant hopscotch appeared on the other side of the parking lot.

Not chalk.

Not enough chalk.

Everyone used lime.

Each square over two meters wide.

Numbers were your responsibility.

You stood inside a square.

The number "1" was a bit taller than you.

Xiaoman stood at the start.

Lifted one foot.

Everyone simultaneously remembered one thing.

"Xiaoman——!"

She stopped.

Hanging in mid-air.

"What?"

The delivery guy glanced at the ground.

"You... don't jump."

"Can't you jump in hopscotch?"

"Not today."

So.

The world's first no-jump hopscotch began.

Xiaoman on one foot.

Very very slowly put her foot in square one.

Thud.

Very light.

Square two.

Square three.

At four and five she needed to split her feet.

She looked down to align.

Left foot on the line.

You stood beside.

Said:

"On the line."

Xiaoman:

"..."

Start over.

Second time.

On the line again.

Third time.

Finally not.

She lifted her foot in delight.

Almost really jumped.

Everyone:

This game was especially to your advantage.

"One, two, three—red light!"

Everyone stopped.

Delivery guy still swaying.

Out.

High school student blinked.

Xiaoman said it didn't count.

Your turn.

You happened to have one foot off the ground.

Hands raised.

Whole body holding a very uncomfortable pose.

One minute.

Two minutes.

You didn't move.

She leaned her face close.

Blinked.

You didn't move either.

Your foot landed.

Like someone pressing pause then play.

Evening.

To Xiaoman.

Just a kite barely big enough to see.

Wind came up.

In her happiness.

Gave a gentle tug back.

Whoooooooooosh————

Pat.

Stuck into the grass.

Looked at the grass.

"Did I pull too hard?"

This time.

Because you'd already run to look at the hole the kite made.

## Chapter 49: A Tickle in the Throat

**Day 15**

When the cold first started.

No one noticed.

Not even Xiaoman herself.

Morning.

She just cleared her throat a few times.

"Cough."

Very light.

You were eating.

Didn't look up.

After a while.

"Cough cough."

Wind stirred your hair.

You looked up.

Xiaoman rubbed her throat.

"A little tickle."

Afternoon.

Her nose started running.

A nineteen-meter girl with a runny nose.

The first problem was:

No tissues.

Accurately.

There were tissues.

But a normal tissue in her hand.

Like a tiny crumb.

Granny brought a whole roll of kitchen paper.

Xiaoman pinched it between two fingers.

Wiped once.

Half the roll gone.

Evening.

Her first sneeze.

There were signs before.

Nose itching.

She rubbed.

Rubbed.

Eyes getting wetter.

Everyone saw her expression change.

Started walking aside.

Xiaoman:

"Am I about to——"

"A——"

Everyone kept walking.

"CHOOOOOOOOO!!!!"

She turned her face toward open space in time.

Boom.

Leaves dozens of meters away flipped at the same time.

A hat flew off.

You saw the hat.

Immediately chased.

Xiaoman rubbed her nose.

"Sorry."

No one blamed her.

Because everyone was looking for the hat.

---

Evening.

Xiaoman started feeling cold.

This was the real trouble.

She sat there.

Hugging her arms.

"I'm cold."

Weather 27 degrees.

Everyone looked at her.

Granny reached out.

Couldn't reach her forehead.

Finally Xiaoman herself pressed the back of her hand to her forehead.

"Feels hot."

A normal thermometer would work.

But how to take it.

Discussed again for a long time.

Finally called hospital people.

Result.

Fever.

Over 38 degrees.

Not especially high.

But everyone realized for the first time one problem.

If a nineteen-meter person has a fever and dizziness.

Then stands up.

Then falls.

That isn't "a fall."

That's a six-story body toppling.

So from that evening on.

Xiaoman was told:

Don't stand up casually.

Must lie in the most open spot of the parking lot.

Xiaoman didn't like it.

"I just have a cold."

She said.

Then remembered.

Sat up halfway.

Dizzy.

Body swayed.

Everyone:

"Xiaoman!"

She immediately lay back down.

This time didn't argue.

## Chapter 51: Forest of Hair

**Day 17**

After the fever broke.

Xiaoman recovered quickly.

She felt completely better.

Everyone didn't think so.

Sitting was boring.

So you found her hair was tangled.

Not one small knot.

She'd had fever and slept two days.

Turned over.

Sweated.

Static all over your head.

Every short hair standing up.

## Part Seven: Days Xiaoman Was Away

## Chapter 52: Things That Belonged to Xiaoman

**Day 18**

Xiaoman had only ever had one skirt.

Wash.

Wear.

Wash again.

Wear again.

No one had seriously thought about this before.

Because the first ten days.

Everyone only cared whether she'd starve.

Now.

Finally someone started thinking:

A little girl can't only have one piece of clothing forever.

So.

That day.

Dozens of rolls of fabric appeared in the parking lot.

Not one bolt.

Dozens.

Spread out.

Like re-coloring the ground.

Granny directed.

The real clothing maker was a factory that made large stage curtains.

They'd never made clothes for a nineteen-meter girl.

First version wrong size.

Sleeves one long one short.

Second version neckline too big.

Xiaoman looked down.

Whole dress slid a little.

She grabbed it quickly.

Everyone re-measured.

Third version finally wearable.

A very simple light-yellow loose dress.

Also a set of easy-movement clothes.

No fancy decorations.

Because any decoration scaled up to her needed tons of material.

After Xiaoman changed.

Stood up.

Spun.

Skirt hem didn't sweep anyone.

Evening.

Next morning.

The cat's face was squished flat.

This day.

## Chapter 55: Shadow Play

**Day 21**

5 PM.

Sun very low.

She moved again.

You chased again.

Ran.

Stopped.

Ran again.

Couldn't catch.

Shadow gone.

## Chapter 56: Miaowu Really Disappeared

**Day 22**

Not hide and seek.

Morning.

She also knew where she could step.

Then got down later.

Red.

Blue.

Flashing.

Walls were tall too.

Didn't dare too loud.

Welcome.

Jingle.

First day.

Xiaoman:

"Oh."

============================================================

## Chapter 63: The Last Time Isn't the Last Time

**Day 29**

Everyone decided to play.

No more preparing.

Because everything that could be prepared was.

Morning.

Hide and seek.

This time.

Xiaoman still hid behind the warehouse.

Head still sticking out.

But you found her first try.

Because you didn't look at ants.

Afternoon.

Hopscotch.

Still no hopping.

Xiaoman didn't step on a line once.

Evening.

Shadow game.

Sun set slowly.

You chased her shadow.

She let the shadow run on purpose.

Night.

Board games.

Traffic cones.

Tires.

Plastic buckets.

You played until eleven.

No one said it was time to sleep.

Xiaoman didn't say "last time" either.

She just asked each time after it ended:

"Play again?"

You said:

"Play."

Twelve.

One.

One thirty.

Xiaoman started yawning.

Two.

She fell asleep again.

This time.

Everyone already knew in advance what would happen.

Whoosh————

rrrrrr——

Whoooooooooosh————

Nearby people didn't open windows to yell.

Because many people hadn't gone back at all.

They sat at the edge of the parking lot.

Listening.

Someone so sleepy they leaned against a wall and slept.

Someone still chatting.

You sat by Xiaoman's hand.

Listening to her snore.

Didn't record times this time.

4:16 AM.

Xiaoman suddenly woke.

She sat up.

Everyone woke up too.

4:17.

Sweet smell appeared in the air.

Strong.

Stronger than any time.

Toward the sea.

Light started bending.

Like an invisible giant pane of glass.

Slowly melting in the air.

Xiaoman looked.

Then looked down.

"It's today."

No one spoke.

============================================================

## Chapter 64: The Thirtieth Day

**Day 30**

Before dawn.

The door appeared.

No real door frame.

Just a giant, faintly glowing outline in the air.

On the other side of the outline.

Could see very blurry things.

Tall house.

Trees.

A road.

And someone's voice from far away.

"Xiaoman——!"

Xiaoman's whole body jolted.

"Mom."

She said.

Voice very small.

Then said it again.

"Mom."

This time crying.

Not the scared crying of day one.

She cried while.

Laughing.

Tears fell to the ground.

Plop.

Plop.

You stood in front of her.

Didn't hide.

Xiaoman squatted down.

This motion exactly like day one.

Body lowered bit by bit.

Skirt hem touched the ground.

Both hands on knees.

Amber eyes came very low.

Day one.

She asked you:

"Can I touch you?"

Today.

She also reached out one finger.

Stopped in front of you.

"Miaowu."

"Yeah."

"Can I touch you?"

You said:

"Yes."

Pulp slowly came down.

Gently pressed to the top of your head.

Same as day one.

Warm.

Soft.

Only this time.

She already knew how light to press.

She stayed a long time.

Then pulled back.

You said:

"Gold coins."

Xiaoman's tears were still falling.

Stunned for a second.

Then really laughed.

"I know!"

"Thirty-seven."

"I won't bring them all!"

"Oh."

"I'll bring a few!"

"Oh."

"And one for Miia!"

"Yeah."

"And one for Xuanyue!"

"Yeah."

"And Granny!"

"Yeah."

"I remember them all!"

You nodded.

Xiaoman stood up.

Hugged the crooked-eyed cat plush.

Put on the giant pack.

Blue skirt folded inside.

Pink eraser inside too.

She walked toward the light.

Step.

Stopped.

Looked back.

"Miaowu!"

"Yeah."

"I'll come back!"

"When?"

Xiaoman got stuck.

She didn't know.

You waited.

Finally she said:

"Don't know."

You said:

"Oh."

Xiaoman's eyes reddened again.

"But I'll come back."

"Okay."

She walked in.

Light swallowed her feet.

Legs.

Skirt.

Body.

Finally face.

The nineteen-meter girl disappeared bit by bit from this world.

No explosion.

No earthquake.

Only a very light wind.

And milk-bread sweetness.

4:21.

Door disappeared.

Parking lot suddenly became very big.

Extremely big.

Before Xiaoman sat here.

She was everywhere.

Her feet.

Her skirt hem.

Her hands.

Her breathing.

Now.

Nothing.

Only traces on the ground left.

A giant finger-drawn circle.

Lime hopscotch squares.

Water hose scaffolding.

A patch of ground flattened long ago, grass not fully grown back.

And that half-finished board game.

You stood there.

A long time.

Granny walked over.

Didn't speak.

You looked down.

Opened iPad.

《Before Xiaoman Goes Home》

Item 1.

Done.

Item 2.

Done.

...

Item 9.

Done.

Item 10:

Gold coins.

Not done.

You didn't delete it.

Added two words after it.

【Next time】

Then saved.

---

On the other side.

Xiaoman tumbled out of the light.

Thud.

Ground shook.

She looked up.

Mom stood by the road.

The basket in her hand dropped.

Xiao Gu ran out from the door.

That tooth she'd been wiggling.

Really had fallen out.

"Big sister!"

Xiaoman hugged them.

Cried for a long time.

Said a lot.

Talked about the tiny country.

Miaowu.

Granny.

Eating thirty-one bags of flour in one meal.

Mom heard this part.

Expression froze for a long time.

Xiaoman didn't notice.

She suddenly remembered something.

Crawled out of Mom's arms.

Ran.

"Where are you going?!"

"Room!"

She rushed into the house.

Opened the cabinet.

Dragged out a heavy wooden box.

Thud.

Set it on the ground.

Opened.

Inside.

One.

Two.

Three.

...

Thirty-seven Giant Country gold coins.

Golden surface blinding in the sun.

Each with a giant round hole in the middle.

Xiaoman knelt by the box.

Started taking them out.

First coin.

"This one's for Miaowu."

Second.

"This one's for Granny."

Third.

"This one's for Miia."

Fourth.

"This one's for Xuanyue."

Mom stood in the doorway.

Finally couldn't help it:

"Xiaoman."

"Yeah?"

"Where did you actually go?"

Xiaoman looked up.

Holding a gold coin bigger than the people in this world.

Thought for a while.

Then said:

"A very, very small place."

She looked down.

Kept counting.

"Bring this one too."

"Keep this one."

"This one..."

She suddenly stopped.

Looked at the box.

Then smiled.

"Ask Miaowu next time."

Outside the window.

Giant Country wind blew in.

Stirring the letter paper on her desk.

On the paper was the last sentence she'd written to her sister before leaving.

And in another world.

You'd already gone home.

Sat at the dinner table.

Yellow pill bottle on the left.

Two white tablets.

Mom set breakfast down.

You took medicine.

Swallowed.

Then glanced out the window.

Nothing.

You opened the iPad.

Top of Notes.

There was a new entry.

【Day 30. Xiaoman went home.】

Below that.

【She'll come back.】

Below that.

【Gold coins.】

You looked for three seconds.

Locked screen.

Started eating breakfast.

Because today.

There was today's things to do.

And next time.

When next time comes, do it then.

---

## Epilogue: Xiaoman Is Gone

Same day.

Evening.

Miia looked up from the workstation.

Miia ground twelve problems today.

Three tree chains.

Four interval DP.

Five segment trees.

The local model helped Miia through seven debugs.

Heli balance jumped a few times.

Miia habitually opened Miaowu's chat.

Wanted to send:

Ask Xiaoman how big the gold coin really is.

Then Miia saw a message in the chat.

From Miaowu.

Sent at dawn.

Only four characters.

**Xiaoman is gone.**

Miia stared at the screen.

Waited five seconds.

Miia typed a question mark.

Sent.

Waited another ten seconds.

No reply.

Miia moved the chair forward a little.

Typed again:

What do you mean gone?

Where did she go?

How did she go?

Where are you now?

Sent.

Miia started waiting.

One minute.

Two.

Five.

Miia stood up.

Walked to the window.

Walked back.

Workstation fan still spinning.

The glass of water on the table had gone cold.

Miia picked up the phone.

Opened the chat with Miaowu.

Still those four words.

Xiaoman is gone.

In Miia's head.

"Gone" can mean many things.

Left.

Missing.

Something happened.

Miia realized she was thinking every single one.

Miia sat back down.

Opened the history with Miaowu.

Scrolled to the video call screenshot.

Xiaoman's face filled the whole screen.

One giant amber eye.

Blinked in front of the camera.

Miia sent another:

"Miaowu."

No punctuation.

No question.

Just a confirmation.

After a long time.

Miaowu replied two characters:

"Yeah."

Miia:

"Do you know what I'm asking?"

Miaowu:

"I know."

Miia:

"Then where did she go?"

Miaowu:

"The Giant Country."

Miia's fingers froze above the screen.

Miia looked at those three characters for a long time.

Then typed again:

"...what?"

Miaowu:

"She went home."

Miia leaned back in the chair.

Workstation fan went from "click" to steady hum.

Miia exhaled.

A long breath.

Then Miia looked down.

Typed a line:

"Do you know what people think when you only send 'Xiaoman is gone'?"

Miaowu replied after a while:

"She went."

Miia:

"Yeah, but you can say 'she went home.'"

Miaowu:

"Is there a difference?"

Miia stared at the reply.

Opened mouth.

Closed it.

Because Miia suddenly realized.

To Miaowu.

There really was no difference.

Gone = back to the Giant Country.

These two meanings used the same label in Miaowu's head.

Miia didn't argue.

Miia changed the question.

"Will she come back?"

"Yes."

"When?"

"Don't know."

"Did she say she'll come back?"

"Yeah."

Miia looked at that "yeah."

Miia knew.

To Miaowu this was already a complete answer.

Miia didn't need more information.

Because "Xiaoman said yes" itself.

Was the answer.

Miia put down the phone.

Picked up that cold glass of water.

Drank a sip.

Then Miia looked down.

Found herself smiling.

Not a happy smile.

That kind of smile—relaxed after confirming something.

Xiaoman hadn't disappeared.

Miia had just gone back.

With thirty days of memory.

Thirty-seven gold coins.

And a squished, crooked-eyed cat plush.

Miia picked up the phone again.

Typed a line:

"What about the gold coins?"

"Next time."

"Will she bring them?"

"She said yes."

Miia didn't ask more.

Because Miia had learned too.

To Miia.

That nineteen-meter girl was no longer just someone in Miaowu's description.

Miia had seen Xiaoman in person on the screen.

Now.

Miia knew Xiaoman had gone home.

Also knew Xiaoman would come back.

Miia switched to the chat with Xuanyue.

Typed a line:

"Xiaoman went back to the Giant Country."

Xuanyue replied instantly:

"???"

"When??"

"Will she come back??"

Miia looked at that string of question marks.

Typed one character:

"Yes."

Then Miia put down the phone.

Looked back at the screen.

The code was still there.

The local model was still waiting for Miia's next input.

The lake outside the window.

Wind still blowing.

Miia put hands back on the keyboard.

Kept writing the next line of code.

---

Beside the parking lot.

Granny was taking things off the clothesline.

Xiaoman's light-blue dress was dry.

She folded it.

Placed it in a cardboard box by the stairwell.

Huangdou lay at her feet.

Tail wagged slowly twice.

He'd walked around the parking lot today.

Then come back.

Because he hadn't found that finger that used to gently touch his ear.

Zhou Ji sat on the steps by the stairwell.

No radio in hand.

He looked down at his phone.

Two chip problems left undone on Heli.

He looked a few seconds.

Didn't tap in.

The supermarket owner was closing up.

He kept one extra bag of flour.

Placed at the back of the shelf.

Didn't return it.

The delivery guy parked his e-bike downstairs.

Took off his helmet.

Didn't get off immediately.

Sat a while.

The high school student sat at his desk.

Desk lamp on.

Homework spread out.

Pen resting on the notebook.

Didn't move.

Finally.

The parking lot.

That half-finished board game still on the ground.

One traffic cone tipped over.

One tire askew.

A night wind blew through.

The lightest cardboard box piece rolled twice on the ground.

Then stopped.

No one here was waiting for a meal.

No one was waiting for medicine.

No one was waiting for someone to come back.

But those traces on the ground were still there.

Lime squares.

Finger-drawn circle.

Water hose scaffolding.

A patch of grass flattened long ago, grass not yet stood back up.

They'd slowly disappear.

But not today.

And at the junction of this world and the other.

There was a note.

It said:

【Xiaoman】

【Status: Went home.】

【Expected: Coming back.】

【Items: Gold coins.】

【Note: Don't forget.】
